2026-11-16 UTC — Railway dev WEB4 native listener audit on deployment 0419877c-170e-4b23-8d8a-ee37393c2b63 confirmed Holochain 0.7.1, original running cell d759f63d0082e1d3, and the wallet-guard coordinator. Admin inventory and the container TCP socket table showed 135 configured app listeners: 134 without an app binding with zero established connections at capture, and one bound to oasis with one established connection. The versioned upstream AdminRequest API exposes AttachAppInterface and ListAppInterfaces, persists listeners across restart, and provides no detach operation. Cleanup remains open. A version-matched offline migration or upstream detach operation must be validated with a rollback and tested on disposable state before applying to the mounted conductor. No live state was modified and no local artifacts were created. [Holochain 0.7.1 Admin API](https://docs.rs/holochain_conductor_api/0.7.1/holochain_conductor_api/enum.AdminRequest.html#variant.AttachAppInterface).

13 November Cancun time. Traced the missing native diagnostics to deployed HoloNET pin `9f897b7ace60`. On Linux, `HoloNETClientBase.StartHolochainConductorAsync` redirects and drains both native output streams into a bounded private buffer. It includes the buffer in a warning only when the admin port fails to open. The teaching cell times out after the admin port opens, so turning on HoloNET console logging alone cannot expose this failure. The managed WEB4 readiness path needs a bounded, secret-reviewed native diagnostic from the process owner before it tears down the conductor. This is a diagnostic gap in COSMIC's conductor path. It does not establish the cause of the cell failure. No native process, download or local build was started.

11 November Firebase versioning audit. The adapter has no persisted history for holons, avatars or avatar details. A version argument currently reaches a live-row query, including username, email, metadata and owned-export selectors. The existing ETag avatar deletion already demonstrates the native conditional-write entry point. Firebase REST permits conditional PUT and DELETE and does not provide conditional PATCH. A GET followed by a regular multi-row PATCH cannot guarantee that the archived row is the one the write replaced. The history design must make previous-row capture, immutable revision insertion and head publication one conditional native operation. Ordinary graph updates must also preserve their all-or-nothing selected-row write. Verify stale-writer rejection, historical identity and lookup values after rename, versioned tombstone visibility, restart persistence, legacy head access and exact cleanup on RTDB before counting history as accepted. [Firebase conditional writes](https://firebase.google.com/docs/database/rest/save-data#updating-data-with-conditional-requests)

11 November Firebase ordinary graph read candidate. GUID and provider-key point reads, all-holon, parent and metadata reads now expand physical children through the existing indexed RTDB parent query. They apply recursion and depth limits, exclude tombstones and reject cyclic links. Clipped selections are marked incomplete. The native CI test asserts root-only, immediate, clipped and recursive reads, parent and metadata expansion, and tombstone exclusion. An independent RTDB GET verifies the stored parent links and the absence of embedded child objects. Historical reads and hosted verification remain open.

11 November Firebase row projection candidate. The ordinary graph save had serialized the runtime Children and AllChildren navigation properties inside the parent as well as writing selected child rows. The shared Firebase holon row serializer now excludes embedded child and parent navigation objects and reads legacy rows using the same contract. The persisted parentHolonId remains the physical relationship. The acknowledgement regression requires the parent row to omit both child navigation properties. Native verification is pending and graph expansion and historical reads remain open.

11 November Firebase read contract audit. The ordinary GUID point read validates and returns a single stored row. Parent loads use the indexed parentHolonId field and return direct matches. Point, all, parent and metadata reads currently ignore child expansion, recursion, depth and requested version. The graph-save candidate only addresses writes. Follow-up acceptance needs independent assertions for root-only, immediate children, clipped and unrestricted depth, deleted-child exclusion and explicit version behavior. The native fixture already installs the parentHolonId index. The official REST documentation confirms PATCH returns the updated data written, and the queued native job remains the evidence gate for actual acknowledgement and cleanup. [Firebase REST write contract](https://firebase.google.com/docs/database/rest/save-data#updating-data-with-patch)

Run 43090501206 passed compilation and the four focused activation cases.

11 November Valkey and Dragonfly evidence reconciliation. Development run 43059249089 at d93d984b7b passed four native avatar/holon smoke cases for each provider and separate encrypted CQL restart/federation jobs. The shared 52-case native lifecycle remains open for both. No tests were repeated.

11 November Firebase Realtime Database storage candidate. [CI run 43090501206](https://github.com/NextGenSoftwareUK/OASIS/actions/runs/43090501206) stopped at graph save.

Both public BNB mainnet wallet balance modes passed through unsigned Nethereum, rejected chain 97 and left storage inactive in [run 43087353377](https://github.com/NextGenSoftwareUK/OASIS/actions/runs/43087353377).

11 November Memcached graph candidate. The native runtime-dependency run 43073793620 is pending.

11 November ActivityPub run 43043728770 at 1234c1270f passed all three managed native checks on Mastodon 4.4.28. The provider matched all 42 authenticated accounts across two pages and canonical point projection. Both shipping deletion entry points acknowledged the exact source text and native reads independently confirmed each private status absent. All fixture containers, network and directory were removed. 11 November ActivityPub run 43042831640 returned all 42 fixture accounts over two populated authenticated pages. Provider and native paging matched, then the account point check hit HTTP 403 because its token lacked `read:accounts`. Both native DELETEs returned HTTP 200, but the adapter treated their source-text acknowledgement as a read projection and required a rendered content field. The adapter now validates the native DELETE shape and records its exact source text. The test token explicitly requests the scope needed by the point check. Final managed acceptance is pending.

11 November ActivityPub run 43042154202 booted Mastodon over TLS and reached authenticated native search. The second page was empty before the managed tests started. Mastodon's create callback overrides an assigned approval flag when registrations are closed, and native SQL omits unapproved users. The fixture now calls Mastodon's approval method after creation and verifies approval and confirmation. The synthetic accounts receive distinct native search ranks for stable offsets. Managed search and owned status deletion remain open.

11 November ActivityPub run 43041663800 loaded the native database schema and stopped while Mastodon validated the synthetic account's email domain. The setup process now configures Mastodon's own allowlist for the reserved fixture domain. The same isolated service also provisions a separate scoped status token so sync and async provider deletion can verify exact acknowledgement and independent native absence for two owned private statuses. Authenticated search and deletion results remain pending.

11 November ActivityPub authenticated search. The new CI workflow starts the official Mastodon release with PostgreSQL, Redis and loopback TLS. It creates 42 synthetic searchable accounts and a scoped user token on that disposable instance, requires a non-empty second native page, then runs the shipping provider's existing full-page and canonical point-projection test. COSMIC public read and browser evidence is reused. The private CI search, separate hosted identity and owned status writes remain unverified.

11 November Polkadot run 43038744304 compiled the shipping provider after removing the unused BootLoader dependency and reached the managed lifecycle. The fresh runner now gives each mode 18 minutes and reports the failed assertion contract, completed-check count and deadline state. The retained fixture's deadline remains ten minutes. Both managed modes still need a successful run.

11 November Polkadot run 43037729937 verified the private genesis, finalized synthetic funding at block 1 and deployed the rebuilt records contract with code hash `0x1033661ac736bcc21e1d53a516f94c580f7e6e50a930e7acb90d519aeea0603d` and transaction `0x754f640ac38cf6283922356f74c4fe9a8b82cc39294662b89bfac43ba4461f7a`. 11 November EOSIO native storage. GitHub Actions run 43026282638 at 778bd23062 provisions a fresh, loopback-only Antelope Spring 1.2.2 chain, deploys the verified OASIS ABI and WASM, and runs the shipping avatar, holon and avatar-detail cases with independent chain table checks. No provider tests ran. Run 43026564273 showed Spring was still initializing when cleos tried to create the account. The readiness condition allowed an empty curl response to pass through jq, and export masked chain ID extraction errors. Run 43026743854 at 90e2fc4c23 requires a successful nonempty response, a produced block and a validated chain ID before signing.

11 November Neon native compute verification. [GitHub Actions](https://github.com/NextGenSoftwareUK/OASIS/actions/runs/43018535989) at 831df99337 attempts the official pinned Neon Compose topology on a disposable Linux runner. The existing sync and async suite accepts an explicitly enabled loopback compute only when it loads the native Neon extension and uses the dedicated test database. Local and hosted reports are isolated. Hosted pooled TLS and service acceptance remain open. The initial run stopped before compute startup because the upstream MinIO client image could not be pulled. The fixture now uses Neon local filesystem remote storage while retaining its official pageserver, safekeepers and compute. The test also requires a native Neon timeline ID. The filesystem-backed run 43019083510 progressed to the compute image build, where the Debian HTTP mirror returned 404 for package versions advertised by its indexes. HTTPS refresh in run 43019547380 returned the same missing Debian packages. The fixture now creates its tenant and timeline through the native pageserver API from the runner, then starts the pinned official compute image directly with the upstream compute specification. This removes the fixture wrapper package install. Direct startup run 43020107245 reached the pageserver but its non-root process could not create the tenant directory on the fixture bind mount. The next revision assigns only that disposable mount to the UID and GID reported by the pinned image. The failed run removed its five containers and network. No local images or build outputs were created.

11 November IPFS separate-process publication. [GitHub Actions](https://github.com/NextGenSoftwareUK/OASIS/actions/runs/43010626932) at c4b07b1f06 starts two writer test hosts with distinct process IDs and synchronizes them before they save to one disposable Kubo repository and DNA path. A third test host reads the persisted catalogue and checks all eight record names. Task revision 57da4b22ef holds an exclusive file lease across persisted catalogue admission and publication. It waits only for known sharing contention and returns an error after 30 seconds of contention. The lease is released when the file handle closes. The [updated native run](https://github.com/NextGenSoftwareUK/OASIS/actions/runs/43010855757) passed both writer processes and the reader. All eight records remained visible. Development 9aaed91520 contains the verified provider and test. Baseline job 130489021464 confirmed both writer tests passed, then the reader failed because the second writer's records were missing. 11 November IPFS independent writers. This verifies a fresh repository through a graceful daemon restart; historical fixture recovery and concurrent publication remain open.

## Phase and rules

Active checkpoint, 10 November 2026. Current HyperDrive verification is split between retained native provider evidence and open isolated fixture gates. Development contains the scoped SQL Server avatar/history and graph-read batch at af621f3c3e. Development also contains the concurrent avatar and avatar-detail history regressions at 2a91bda8eb. Both passed separately on LocalDB and compiled together with Development Core 0fc929336 (zero errors). BigQuery hosted lifecycle verification awaits its isolated fixture. HyperDrive routing verification follows provider acceptance.

- Breadth first: real provider implementations and canonical core-storage-v2 (26checks per sync/async mode), approximately one day per family. Non-storage uses capability-v1, not storage tests.
- All applicable provider baselines precede global failover/load balancing/replication verification, then priority-provider depth. No Polkadot/NEAR/SQLite depth now.
- Use the existing C:/Source/OASIS2-hyperdrive worktree on work/hyperdrive-provider-completion. No new clones/worktrees/environments; reuse cached runtimes.
- Stage exact owned files, preserve concurrent dirty work/submodules, no force pushes/attribution trailers. Preserve the separate COSMIC/CQL checkout and branch.

| Provider | Evidence to carry forward | Next acceptance gate |

| Neon | Hosted conformance project compiled. | Isolated hosted Neon database for native sync and async lifecycle. |

The current process exposes none of the hosted fixture variables listed in [Actions for David](../Release%20and%20Status/ACTIONS-FOR-DAVID.md). Keep fixture access there and preserve existing native receipts. COSMIC tracks separate teaching-source acceptance for Neon (direct compute Npgsql reader), Convex (indexed exact-ID companion) and Supabase (bounded PostgREST reader). Preserve those results in the COSMIC scope. HyperDrive requires its own disposable writable fixtures for the lifecycle runs above. The same separation applies to BigQuery, where COSMIC requires a managed read-only teaching fixture.

## Key commands

```powershell
git status --short
git fetch origin
python -B Scripts/Providers/update_provider_matrix.py
python -B Scripts/Providers/test_provider_matrix.py
python -B Scripts/Providers/test_conformance_reports.py
```
Wrap producer/consumer builds once in Scripts/TestHosts/Invoke-WithProviderBuildLease.ps1; use cached restores and bounded workers. Native runners require original configuration/report review; do not reset failed fixtures to obtain a pass.

13 November Cancun time. Azure Cosmos DB durable replication audit: the shipping holon repository resolves each current holon ID to its own logical partition, and the factory provisions holonItems with /id. Existing history can share the root partition. A native batch for one root cannot establish atomicity across arbitrary graph roots and a shared receipt or outbox. The provider has no bound atomic source or committed replica receiver implementation. Full durable graph replication requires a storage layout and migration that preserve current point routing and history while giving the graph, receipt and obligations one atomic commit scope. The emulator routing passes cover the existing routing paths. They do not qualify durable bound graph writes.

13 November Cancun time. After checking the COSMIC status, extended the Oracle Free and SQLite native fixture to cover a healthy Oracle miss by provider key. The test requires RecordNotFound and zero SQLite requests while SQLite holds the exact record. Refreshed the workflow comparison baseline to the current Development revision because it already includes the prior ID-only regression. Native CI is pending.

13 November Cancun time. Oracle durable replication review. The shipping ODP.NET conditional writer already holds the operation guard, graph writes and receipt in one Oracle transaction. Durable outbound obligations can be inserted before that commit. Oracle currently exposes conditional storage only, with no native store binding, committed replica receiver or bound dispatcher outbox. Preserve the existing read-only schema validation used by restricted accounts when introducing replication schema. The provider-key candidate ca0aae1d0752 is staged on Development 72ffc86c2082 and [native CI](https://github.com/NextGenSoftwareUK/OASIS/actions/runs/43539563461/job/132276668079) is queued. All seven APIs on the baseline reported success.

14 November Cancun time. OPORTAL performance investigation. A clean Chromium run of the public signed-out /beamin route recorded 189 ms document TTFB, 852 ms first contentful paint, and 1,401 ms for the full component-ready event.
